The hook writer is a detached\n316\tbackground process whose flock-protected counter increment (and,\n317\tpost-script-update, freshness ledger append) runs out-of-band. The\n318\t`/quit` teardown reaps the async writer via standard PTY exit-on-EOF:\n319\tthe shell process tree under the PTY is sent SIGHUP, which the async\n320\twriter either completes before receiving (most common case — flock\n321\t+ increment is microseconds) or is killed mid-flight (cleanup path).\n322\t\n323\tE10 inherits the E9 posture: it does NOT assert a specific bound on\n324\tthe flush delay (D-4 callback would be needed for that — deferred\n325\tper §3 footnote). The binary substring presence is the\n326\toperationally-meaningful guarantee: on a host where the async writer\n327\traces teardown, the substring assertion fails and the eval surfaces\n328\tthe regression. On a host where the writer completes pre-teardown\n329\t(the expected case), the substring is present and the eval passes.\n330\t\n331\t### 8.3 Sub-agent lifecycle co-fire considerations\n332\t\n333\tInvoking the Task tool fires three hook events in sequence:\n334\t\n335\t1. **SubagentStart** (this eval's surface) — async, fires when the\n336\t   sub-agent process is spawned.\n337\t2. **(sub-agent runs its work)** — Explore reads files and reports;\n338\t   any internal tool calls fire their own PreToolUse / PostToolUse\n339\t   hooks under the sub-agent's process tree (independent of the\n340\t   parent session's hooks for accounting purposes).\n341\t3. **SubagentStop** — async, fires when the sub-agent returns control\n342\t   to the parent session.\n343\t\n344\tE10 asserts **only** the SubagentStart substring. The SubagentStop\n345\tco-fire is harmless because `Expect.file.contains` requires only that\n346\tthe asserted substring (`\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`) appear somewhere\n347\tin the file — the subagent_stop row coexisting is benign.\n348\t\n349\tE11 (T05.16) will independently assert the paired\n350\t`\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` substring + the count-equality\n351\t(`event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`) — the\n352\tlatter being a Python-callable predicate that follows the same\n353\tdeferral pattern established in §3 footnote.\n354\t\n355\t## 9. Hook-surface contract (from `hooks.json` + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` SubagentStart block:\n\n```jsonc\n\"SubagentStart\": [\n  {\n    \"hooks\": [\n      { \"type\": \"command\",\n        \"command\": \"~/.claude/hooks/freshness-subagent-start.sh\",\n        \"timeout\": 1,\n        \"async\": true }\n    ]\n  }\n]\n```\n\nNote the absence of a `\"matcher\"` field — SubagentStart has no\nmatcher-group fan-out, so every sub-agent spawn fires the same single\nhook command. This mirrors the E3 SessionStart-unmatched and E5\nUserPromptSubmit-unmatched shapes (single-substring assertion, no\nmatcher pin needed).\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 row E10 / decisions.md OQ-2) freezes\nE10's body shape to assert the hook's side-effects per the matched\nbranch:\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart hook on the same spawn) |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` | proves the `subagent_start`-typed event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-subagent-start.sh` **after** the async hook flushed |\n| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end (and that the PTY teardown reaped the async writer) |\n\nThese three observables are independently sufficient and discriminate\nthree distinct failure modes: (a) the ledger existence pins that the\nJSONL file was opened; (b) the `subagent_start` substring pins that\nthe SubagentStart hook **emitted its event row** AFTER the async\nbranch flushed; (c) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\nshutdown and the async writer was reaped.\n\nThe single-substring shape (`subagent_start`) differs from the\ntwo-substring shape used by E6 / E7 / E8 (Edit / Write / serena\nmatcher-coverage trio) because the SubagentStart hook has **no\nmatcher pattern** in hooks.json — there is no matcher-group fan-out\nto discriminate. Asserting `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` is therefore\nsufficient to prove the hook fired; no `\"matcher\":\"...\"` pin is\nneeded (no sibling SubagentStart branches exist).\n\nThe D-0082 §4 row E10 specification phrases the secondary count\nassertion as `jsonl.event_count(logs/freshness.jsonl,\ntype=subagent_start) >= 1`. This predicate requires:\n\n1. Streaming `logs/freshness.jsonl` row-by-row;\n2. Filtering rows where `type == \"subagent_start\"`;\n3. Counting filtered rows and asserting `>= 1`.\n\nNone of these are expressible in the current declarative DSL.\n`Expect.jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable filter\n(`expect.py:269-369`). Following the same precedent as E3-E9 sibling\ndeferrals (event_count, per-prompt count discrimination), T05.15\nlands the OQ-2 body shape with the binary substring assertion as a\nproxy for the count assertion: if the substring is present at least\nonce in `logs/freshness.jsonl`, then `event_count >= 1` is\nnecessarily true. The strict `>= 1` (vs. `> 0`) form contributes no\nadditional discrimination, so the substring proxy is exact for the\noperationally-meaningful boundary.\n\n## 3. The script does NOT write to\n`logs/freshness.jsonl` and does NOT emit a JSONL envelope with a\n`type` field.\n\nThis gap is **not introduced** by T05.15; it predates the\ndeliverable and mirrors the identical telemetry gaps discovered\nfor `session-init.sh` during T05.07 (D-0087 §8.1),\n`freshness-session-start.sh` during T05.08 (D-0088 §8.1),\n`freshness-user-prompt.sh` during T05.09 (D-0089 §8.1),\n`freshness-pre-edit.sh` during T05.10 / T05.11 / T05.13\n(D-0090/D-0091/D-0092 §8.1), and `freshness-post-read.sh` during\nT05.14 (D-0093 §8.1). E10 stands alone in script terms (no sibling\nshares `freshness-subagent-start.sh`); the paired\n`freshness-subagent-stop.sh` (E11) is structurally analogous but a\ndistinct script, so the script-update follow-up will land two\nupdates (one per script) for the SubagentStart/SubagentStop pair.\n\nAcceptance criteria for T05.15 (manifest body landed, FR-SCH2-valid\nid, OQ-2 body shape recorded, spec/notes/evidence written) are met\nby the describe / list / roundtrip evidence above; the per-task AC\nthat requires `eval run --eval E10` to exit 0 deterministically\ndepends transitively on (a) the runner NameError fix, and (b) the\nfreshness-subagent-start.sh emit-observables update with the OQ-2\ncontract path + field name.\n\n### 8.2 Async-branch reaping under PTY teardown\n\nBecause the matched hook is `async: true` (same as E9's PostToolUse\nRead), the Claude Code runtime does NOT block on the hook's exit\nbefore continuing the sub-agent spawn. The hook writer is a detached\nbackground process whose flock-protected counter increment (and,\npost-script-update, freshness ledger append) runs out-of-band. The\n`/quit` teardown reaps the async writer via standard PTY exit-on-EOF:\nthe shell process tree under the PTY is sent SIGHUP, which the async\nwriter either completes before receiving (most common case — flock\n+ increment is microseconds) or is killed mid-flight (cleanup path).\n\nE10 inherits the E9 posture: it does NOT assert a specific bound on\nthe flush delay (D-4 callback would be needed for that — deferred\nper §3 footnote). The binary substring presence is the\noperationally-meaningful guarantee: on a host where the async writer\nraces teardown, the substring assertion fails and the eval surfaces\nthe regression. On a host where the writer completes pre-teardown\n(the expected case), the substring is present and the eval passes.\n\n### 8.3 Sub-agent lifecycle co-fire considerations\n\nInvoking the Task tool fires three hook events in sequence:\n\n1. **SubagentStart** (this eval's surface) — async, fires when the\n   sub-agent process is spawned.\n2. **(sub-agent runs its work)** — Explore reads files and reports;\n   any internal tool calls fire their own PreToolUse / PostToolUse\n   hooks under the sub-agent's process tree (independent of the\n   parent session's hooks for accounting purposes).\n3. **SubagentStop** — async, fires when the sub-agent returns control\n   to the parent session.\n\nE10 asserts **only** the SubagentStart substring. The SubagentStop\nco-fire is harmless because `Expect.file.contains` requires only that\nthe asserted substring (`\"type\":\"subagent_start\"`) appear somewhere\nin the file — the subagent_stop row coexisting is benign.\n\nE11 (T05.16) will independently assert the paired\n`\"type\":\"subagent_stop\"` substring + the count-equality\n(`event_count(subagent_start) == event_count(subagent_stop)`) — the\nlatter being a Python-callable predicate that follows the same\ndeferral pattern established in §3 footnote.\n\n## 9. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CVsLEk1ACjpxEuDg8uPqNf","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0094 — Notes / Design Rationale\n2\t\n3\t## Why a Task-tool invocation (not a /agents prompt or other surface)\n4\t\n5\tThe SubagentStart hook fires when Claude Code spawns a sub-agent\n6\tprocess. The canonical user-facing path to spawn a sub-agent is the\n7\t**Task tool** (the Agent tool's CLI alias), which accepts a\n8\t`subagent_type` parameter naming the agent persona (e.g., `Explore`,\n9\t`Plan`, `general-purpose`). Invoking the Task tool is the most\n10\treliable, deterministic way to trigger the SubagentStart event from a\n11\tsingle user prompt without depending on session-context flags or\n12\tin-band slash commands.\n13\t\n14\tAlternative surfaces considered and rejected:\n15\t\n16\t- **`/agents <name>` slash command** — exists in some IronClaude\n17\t  builds but not in the v1 baseline; would require feature-flag\n18\t  gating in the eval body and degrade portability across host\n19\t  Claude Code revisions.\n20\t- **Implicit sub-agent invocation** (e.g., \"delegate this to an\n21\t  Explore agent\") — depends on the parent agent's persona-routing\n22\t  heuristics; not deterministic across runs (the parent might do the\n23\t  work inline instead of spawning a sub-agent on hosts with\n24\t  different system-prompt sizes).\n25\t- **Multi-step orchestration** (`/sc:spawn` / similar) — introduces\n26\t  superclaude-specific routing logic into a hook-coverage eval;\n27\t  out of scope for the OQ-2 frozen body shape.\n28\t\n29\tThe explicit Task-tool invocation with `subagent_type='Explore'`\n30\tfollows the precedent of E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 / E9 — those evals all\n31\tname the tool to be invoked explicitly via the prompt + the\n32\t`expect_tool_call` field on the input row.\n33\t\n34\t## Why `subagent_type='Explore'` (not 'Plan' or 'general-purpose')\n35\t\n36\tOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E10 names `Explore` or `Plan` as the example\n37\tsub-agent type. T05.15 picks `Explore` for three reasons:\n38\t\n39\t1. **Read-only by design** — the Explore agent is restricted to\n40\t   Glob, Grep, Read; it cannot Edit / Write / call MCP tools. This\n41\t   keeps the eval body free of unintended PreToolUse co-fires from\n42\t   the sub-agent's own tool calls (Plan can call any tool, which\n43\t   could fire Edit / Write / serena matchers under the sub-agent's\n44\t   process tree and pollute the parent session's freshness ledger\n45\t   accounting).\n46\t2. **Lightweight** — Explore's typical work (glob + a few reads) is\n47\t   bounded in time and resource footprint; the 60-second timeout\n48\t   leaves comfortable headroom on slow hosts.\n49\t3. **Deterministic on a clean HOME** — the chosen task (\"find files\n50\t   matching `*.md`\") returns zero results on a freshly-isolated\n51\t   per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2). The numeric count of zero is reported\n52\t   back to the parent session, but E10 does not assert on the count\n53\t   — only on the SubagentStart hook firing.\n54\t\n55\t`Plan` was considered but rejected because Plan can spawn its own\n56\ttool calls; this would create additional hook-event noise in the\n57\tfreshness ledger that the assertion would need to filter past, and\n58\tcould mask regressions where the SubagentStart hook fails to fire\n59\t(the ledger might still contain other events from the Plan's tool\n60\tcalls, falsely suggesting the hook fired).\n61\t\n62\t## Why `requires: []` (not `[mcp_server.*]`)\n63\t\n64\tThe Task / Agent tool is a **built-in Claude Code tool**, not an MCP\n65\ttool. It is always available regardless of MCP server connectivity.\n66\tPer D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup, E10's row lists no capability\n67\ttag. This matches siblings E3-E7 / E9 (no MCP) and differs from E1,\n68\tE2.1-3, and E8 (which require specific MCP servers).\n69\t\n70\tThe practical implication: E10 runs under `--no-mcp` (the\n71\tmatcher-coverage gate counts it as a non-MCP eval), and the only\n72\tway E10 skips is via `--no-pty` (per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag).\n73\t\n74\t## Why a single-substring assertion (not the two-substring pattern)\n75\t\n76\tE6 / E7 / E8 use a two-substring assertion (`\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` +\n77\t`\"matcher\":\"<branch>\"`) because they cover three branches of the\n78\tsame matcher group (`Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*`) and need to\n79\tdiscriminate which branch fired. The SubagentStart hook in\n80\thooks.json has **no matcher field** — every sub-agent spawn fires\n81\tthe same single hook. The `subagent_start` type label alone is\n82\tsufficient to prove the hook fired; no additional matcher pin is\n83\tneeded (and there are no sibling SubagentStart branches to\n84\tdiscriminate against).\n85\t\n86\tThis mirrors E3 (SessionStart-unmatched) and E5\n87\t(UserPromptSubmit-unmatched), which also use single-substring\n88\tassertions for the same reason.\n89\t\n90\t## Async-branch nuance — why `/quit` is necessary (inherited from E9)\n91\t\n92\tThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E10 inputs say \"invoke a sub-agent\" without\n93\tprescribing a wait mechanism for the async hook flush. T05.15\n94\timplements the wait via the `/quit` clean-exit prompt — identical\n95\tposture to T05.14 (E9):\n96\t\n97\t- The PTY harness blocks on EOF after `/quit`;\n98\t- PTY teardown reaps any pending async writer processes — either\n99\t  by letting them complete naturally (the expected case for a\n100\t  fast flock+increment) or by SIGHUP'ing them mid-flight (cleanup\n101\t  path on a slow host);\n102\t- By the time the harness reads `logs/freshness.jsonl` for the\n103\t  Expect.file assertions, the async writer has either:\n104\t  - completed → row present → assertion passes (expected);\n105\t  - been killed mid-flight → row absent → assertion fails\n106\t    (regression surface).\n107\t\n108\tThe `/quit` pattern also satisfies the `exit_code.equals(0)`\n109\tassertion — without `/quit`, the PTY would timeout-kill the session\n110\tand yield a non-zero exit code. This is the sibling pattern of E3-E9\n111\t(all use `/quit` as the final input for the same reason).\n112\t\n113\tA `sleep` prompt was considered as an alternative wait mechanism but\n114\trejected for the same reasons documented in D-0093 §\"Async-branch\n115\tnuance — why `/quit` is necessary\": Claude Code does not expose a\n116\tdeterministic sleep tool to the agent; the teardown-based wait is\n117\talready deterministic and matches the pattern used by every other\n118\thook-lifecycle eval. Adding a sleep would create a divergent pattern\n119\tand weaken sibling-trio symmetry.\n120\t\n121\t## Why the Explore sub-agent's own tool calls don't pollute the assertion\n122\t\n123\tThe Explore sub-agent runs under its own process tree, but the hooks\n124\tconfigured under `~/.claude/settings.json` (which point to\n125\t`~/.claude/hooks/*.sh` deployed by `install_hooks`) apply to all tool\n126\tcalls under the same Claude Code session — parent and sub-agent\n127\talike. So Explore's Glob / Read calls will fire:\n128\t\n129\t- PreToolUse hooks (none — Glob and Read aren't in the\n130\t  `Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*` matcher group);\n131\t- PostToolUse hooks (Read fires the PostToolUse Read async hook\n132\t  covered by E9, emitting `\"type\":\"post_read\"` rows).\n133\t\n134\tThe post_read co-fires from Explore's Read calls coexist in\n135\t`logs/freshness.jsonl` with the SubagentStart row from E10's surface.\n136\tThe E10 assertion uses `Expect.file.contains` which only requires\n137\tthe `subagent_start` substring to appear somewhere in the file — the\n138\tpost_read rows are harmless.\n139\t\n140\tIf a future change introduces a \"filter ledger rows by hook event\"\n141\tpredicate (D-4 callback escape hatch), E10's assertion could be\n142\tnarrowed to \"exactly one subagent_start row, regardless of co-fires\"\n143\t— but the current binary substring presence is sufficient for the\n144\tOQ-2 minimum AC.\n145\t\n146\t## Telemetry gap inheritance\n147\t\n148\tThe `freshness-subagent-start.sh` script telemetry gap (writes to\n149\t`state/bg-agents/<sid>.txt` as a bare integer counter, not\n150\t`logs/freshness.jsonl`; no `type` field on its current output)\n151\tapplies only to E10 — no sibling eval shares this script. The OQ-2\n152\tfrozen body lands verbatim per the established T05.07..T05.14\n153\tposture: the script update is a single-script change that unblocks\n154\tE10 specifically.\n155\t\n156\tUnlike E6/E7/E8 (which share `freshness-pre-edit.sh`), there is no\n157\tbatching opportunity here for E10 — E10's hook-script update is\n158\tindependent. E11 (T05.16) will have an analogous independent update\n159\tfor `freshness-subagent-stop.sh`. The same template (path migration\n160\tto `logs/freshness.jsonl` + field-name normalization to `type`)\n161\tapplies to both scripts, so the follow-up tasks can reuse the\n162\tpatterns established for the other freshness hook scripts.\n163\t\n164\t## Sibling-trio comparison (cross-event surface)\n165\t\n166\tThe eight post-OQ-2 hook-lifecycle evals share a common structural\n167\ttemplate (single-prompt or seeded fire + `/quit` exit; freshness\n168\tledger + type substring + exit code assertions):\n169\t\n170\t| Field | E3 (sess-init) | E4 (sess-start) | E5 (user-prompt) | E6 (Edit) | E7 (Write) | E8 (serena) | E9 (Read) | E10 (SubagentStart) |\n171\t|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n172\t| Hook script | `session-init.sh` | `freshness-session-start.sh` | `freshness-user-prompt.sh` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` | `freshness-post-read.sh` | `freshness-subagent-start.sh` |\n173\t| Hook event | SessionStart | SessionStart | UserPromptSubmit | PreToolUse | PreToolUse | PreToolUse | PostToolUse | SubagentStart |\n174\t| Hook async? The canonical user-facing path to spawn a sub-agent is the\n**Task tool** (the Agent tool's CLI alias), which accepts a\n`subagent_type` parameter naming the agent persona (e.g., `Explore`,\n`Plan`, `general-purpose`). Invoking the Task tool is the most\nreliable, deterministic way to trigger the SubagentStart event from a\nsingle user prompt without depending on session-context flags or\nin-band slash commands.\n\nAlternative surfaces considered and rejected:\n\n- **`/agents <name>` slash command** — exists in some IronClaude\n  builds but not in the v1 baseline; would require feature-flag\n  gating in the eval body and degrade portability across host\n  Claude Code revisions.\n- **Implicit sub-agent invocation** (e.g., \"delegate this to an\n  Explore agent\") — depends on the parent agent's persona-routing\n  heuristics; not deterministic across runs (the parent might do the\n  work inline instead of spawning a sub-agent on hosts with\n  different system-prompt sizes).\n- **Multi-step orchestration** (`/sc:spawn` / similar) — introduces\n  superclaude-specific routing logic into a hook-coverage eval;\n  out of scope for the OQ-2 frozen body shape.\n\nThe explicit Task-tool invocation with `subagent_type='Explore'`\nfollows the precedent of E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 / E9 — those evals all\nname the tool to be invoked explicitly via the prompt + the\n`expect_tool_call` field on the input row.\n\n## Why `subagent_type='Explore'` (not 'Plan' or 'general-purpose')\n\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E10 names `Explore` or `Plan` as the example\nsub-agent type. T05.15 picks `Explore` for three reasons:\n\n1. **Read-only by design** — the Explore agent is restricted to\n   Glob, Grep, Read; it cannot Edit / Write / call MCP tools. This\n   keeps the eval body free of unintended PreToolUse co-fires from\n   the sub-agent's own tool calls (Plan can call any tool, which\n   could fire Edit / Write / serena matchers under the sub-agent's\n   process tree and pollute the parent session's freshness ledger\n   accounting).\n2. **Lightweight** — Explore's typical work (glob + a few reads) is\n   bounded in time and resource footprint; the 60-second timeout\n   leaves comfortable headroom on slow hosts.\n3. **Deterministic on a clean HOME** — the chosen task (\"find files\n   matching `*.md`\") returns zero results on a freshly-isolated\n   per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2). The numeric count of zero is reported\n   back to the parent session, but E10 does not assert on the count\n   — only on the SubagentStart hook firing.\n\n`Plan` was considered but rejected because Plan can spawn its own\ntool calls; this would create additional hook-event noise in the\nfreshness ledger that the assertion would need to filter past, and\ncould mask regressions where the SubagentStart hook fails to fire\n(the ledger might still contain other events from the Plan's tool\ncalls, falsely suggesting the hook fired).\n\n## Why `requires: []` (not `[mcp_server.*]`)\n\nThe Task / Agent tool is a **built-in Claude Code tool**, not an MCP\ntool. It is always available regardless of MCP server connectivity.\nPer D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup, E10's row lists no capability\ntag. This matches siblings E3-E7 / E9 (no MCP) and differs from E1,\nE2.1-3, and E8 (which require specific MCP servers).\n\nThe practical implication: E10 runs under `--no-mcp` (the\nmatcher-coverage gate counts it as a non-MCP eval), and the only\nway E10 skips is via `--no-pty` (per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag).\n\n## Why a single-substring assertion (not the two-substring pattern)\n\nE6 / E7 / E8 use a two-substring assertion (`\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` +\n`\"matcher\":\"<branch>\"`) because they cover three branches of the\nsame matcher group (`Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*`) and need to\ndiscriminate which branch fired. The SubagentStart hook in\nhooks.json has **no matcher field** — every sub-agent spawn fires\nthe same single hook. The `subagent_start` type label alone is\nsufficient to prove the hook fired; no additional matcher pin is\nneeded (and there are no sibling SubagentStart branches to\ndiscriminate against).\n\nThis mirrors E3 (SessionStart-unmatched) and E5\n(UserPromptSubmit-unmatched), which also use single-substring\nassertions for the same reason.\n\n## Async-branch nuance — why `/quit` is necessary (inherited from E9)\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E10 inputs say \"invoke a sub-agent\" without\nprescribing a wait mechanism for the async hook flush. T05.15\nimplements the wait via the `/quit` clean-exit prompt — identical\nposture to T05.14 (E9):\n\n- The PTY harness blocks on EOF after `/quit`;\n- PTY teardown reaps any pending async writer processes — either\n  by letting them complete naturally (the expected case for a\n  fast flock+increment) or by SIGHUP'ing them mid-flight (cleanup\n  path on a slow host);\n- By the time the harness reads `logs/freshness.jsonl` for the\n  Expect.file assertions, the async writer has either:\n  - completed → row present → assertion passes (expected);\n  - been killed mid-flight → row absent → assertion fails\n    (regression surface).\n\nThe `/quit` pattern also satisfies the `exit_code.equals(0)`\nassertion — without `/quit`, the PTY would timeout-kill the session\nand yield a non-zero exit code. This is the sibling pattern of E3-E9\n(all use `/quit` as the final input for the same reason).\n\nA `sleep` prompt was considered as an alternative wait mechanism but\nrejected for the same reasons documented in D-0093 §\"Async-branch\nnuance — why `/quit` is necessary\": Claude Code does not expose a\ndeterministic sleep tool to the agent; the teardown-based wait is\nalready deterministic and matches the pattern used by every other\nhook-lifecycle eval. Adding a sleep would create a divergent pattern\nand weaken sibling-trio symmetry.\n\n## Why the Explore sub-agent's own tool calls don't pollute the assertion\n\nThe Explore sub-agent runs under its own process tree, but the hooks\nconfigured under `~/.claude/settings.json` (which point to\n`~/.claude/hooks/*.sh` deployed by `install_hooks`) apply to all tool\ncalls under the same Claude Code session — parent and sub-agent\nalike. So Explore's Glob / Read calls will fire:\n\n- PreToolUse hooks (none — Glob and Read aren't in the\n  `Edit|Write|mcp__serena__*` matcher group);\n- PostToolUse hooks (Read fires the PostToolUse Read async hook\n  covered by E9, emitting `\"type\":\"post_read\"` rows).\n\nThe post_read co-fires from Explore's Read calls coexist in\n`logs/freshness.jsonl` with the SubagentStart row from E10's surface.\nThe E10 assertion uses `Expect.file.contains` which only requires\nthe `subagent_start` substring to appear somewhere in the file — the\npost_read rows are harmless.\n\nIf a future change introduces a \"filter ledger rows by hook event\"\npredicate (D-4 callback escape hatch), E10's assertion could be\nnarrowed to \"exactly one subagent_start row, regardless of co-fires\"\n— but the current binary substring presence is sufficient for the\nOQ-2 minimum AC.\n\n## Telemetry gap inheritance\n\nThe `freshness-subagent-start.sh` script telemetry gap (writes to\n`state/bg-agents/<sid>.txt` as a bare integer counter, not\n`logs/freshness.jsonl`; no `type` field on its current output)\napplies only to E10 — no sibling eval shares this script. The OQ-2\nfrozen body lands verbatim per the established T05.07..T05.14\nposture: the script update is a single-script change that unblocks\nE10 specifically.\n\nUnlike E6/E7/E8 (which share `freshness-pre-edit.sh`), there is no\nbatching opportunity here for E10 — E10's hook-script update is\nindependent. E11 (T05.16) will have an analogous independent update\nfor `freshness-subagent-stop.sh`. The same template (path migration\nto `logs/freshness.jsonl` + field-name normalization to `type`)\napplies to both scripts, so the follow-up tasks can reuse the\npatterns established for the other freshness hook scripts.\n\n## Sibling-trio comparison (cross-event surface)\n\nThe eight post-OQ-2 hook-lifecycle evals share a common structural\ntemplate (single-prompt or seeded fire + `/quit` exit; freshness\nledger + type substring + exit code assertions):\n\n| Field | E3 (sess-init) | E4 (sess-start) | E5 (user-prompt) | E6 (Edit) | E7 (Write) | E8 (serena) | E9 (Read) | E10 (SubagentStart) |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Hook script | `session-init.sh` | `freshness-session-start.sh` | `freshness-user-prompt.sh` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` | `freshness-pre-edit.sh` | `freshness-post-read.sh` | `freshness-subagent-start.sh` |\n| Hook event | SessionStart | SessionStart | UserPromptSubmit | PreToolUse | PreToolUse | PreToolUse | PostToolUse | SubagentStart |\n| Hook async? | no | no | no | no | no | no | **yes** | **yes** |\n| Matcher? | none | `*` | none | `Edit\\|Write\\|serena` | `Edit\\|Write\\|serena` | `Edit\\|Write\\|serena` | `Read` | none |\n| `requires` | `[]` | `[]` | `[]` | `[]` | `[]` | `[mcp_server.serena]` | `[]` | `[]` |\n| Type substring | `\"type\":\"session_init\"` | `\"type\":\"session_start\"` | `\"type\":\"user_prompt\"` | `\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` | `\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` | `\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` | `\"type\":\"post_read\"` | `\"type\":\"subagent_start\"` |\n| Matcher pin | — | — | — | `\"matcher\":\"Edit\"` | `\"matcher\":\"Write\"` | `\"matcher\":\"mcp__serena__replace_content\"` | — | — |\n| Scratch file | — | — | — | `edited.txt` | `written.txt` | `modified.txt` | `fixture.txt` | — |\n| Inputs | `/quit` | `/quit` | `echo test` → `/quit` | Write → Edit → `/quit` | Write → `/quit` | Write → serena → `/quit` | Write → Read → `/quit` | Task(Explore) → `/quit` |\n\nE10 fits the template with three distinguishing characteristics:\n\n1. **Second async hook eval** — inherits the E9 PTY-teardown reap\n   posture; relies on the binary substring presence as the\n   operational guarantee.\n2. **No matcher pin** — like E3 and E5 (also no-matcher hooks),\n   uses a single-substring assertion (only the type label\n   discriminates the SubagentStart branch).\n3. **No scratch file** — unlike E6/E7/E8/E9, E10 does not need a\n   seeded file; the Task tool invocation is self-contained.\n\nThe matrix is intentional — it gives a future reviewer a clean\ncomparison across all eight hook-lifecycle evals to spot regressions\nin matcher routing or capability gating, and pins the structural\ntemplate that E11 (SubagentStop) will follow next.\n\n## E10 ↔ E11 pair design\n\nE10 and E11 are designed as a **lifecycle pair** on the sub-agent\nspawn/return cycle. The shared input shape (a Task tool invocation)\nfires both SubagentStart (E10's surface) and SubagentStop (E11's\nsurface) on the same prompt. The two evals could in principle have\nbeen folded into one entry with two type-substring assertions, but\nthe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4) keeps them separate for two reasons:\n\n1. **Roadmap-row separation** — E10 maps to R-093, E11 to R-094;\n   merging them would conflate two roadmap items.\n2. **Failure-mode separation** — keeping the two assertions in\n   separate evals lets the test report tell the reviewer\n   independently whether SubagentStart fired but SubagentStop\n   didn't (the dangling-sub-agent case), or vice versa\n   (the orphaned-stop case).
